  157: # _pagination($self) -> ($page, $per_page, $offset)
  158: #
  159: # Reads ?page= and ?per_page= from the current request, clamps them to sane
  160: # ranges, and returns the 1-based page number, the page size, and the
  161: # 0-based row offset.  Pagination is optional: when neither param is present
  162: # per_page defaults to DEFAULT_PER_PAGE.

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  163: sub _pagination {
  164: 	my $self = $_[0];
  165: 
  166: 	my $per_page = int($self->param('per_page') || $DEFAULT_PER_PAGE);
  167: 	$per_page = 1         if $per_page < 1;
  168: 	$per_page = $MAX_PER_PAGE if $per_page > $MAX_PER_PAGE;
  169: 	my $page = int($self->param('page') || 1);
  170: 	$page = 1 if $page < 1;
  171: 	my $offset = ($page - 1) * $per_page;
  172: 	return ($page, $per_page, $offset);
  173: }

  187: # _is_safe_url($url) -> bool
  188: #
  189: # Purpose: SSRF guard.  Blocks the most dangerous classes of Server-Side Request
  190: #          Forgery targets: loopback aliases (localhost, 127/8) and literal
  191: #          private/link-local/CGNAT IPv4 addresses in the URL host component.
  192: #
  193: # Design rationale for scope:

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  194: #   Resolving hostnames via DNS and checking the result is ineffective: a
  195: #   separate DNS lookup happens at LWP connect time (TOCTOU / DNS rebinding
  196: #   window).  The authoritative defence against hostname-based SSRF is a
  197: #   network-layer egress firewall.  This function handles the Perl-layer

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  198: #   interception for the most common patterns that operators cannot easily
  199: #   filter at the network level: bare loopback aliases and literal private IPs
  200: #   hard-coded by an attacker.
  201: #
  202: # Blocked targets:
  203: #   localhost / 127.0.0.0/8 / 0.0.0.0 / ::1  -- loopback aliases
  204: #   10.0.0.0/8    -- RFC 1918 private (literal IP)
  205: #   172.16.0.0/12 -- RFC 1918 private (literal IP)

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  206: #   192.168.0.0/16-- RFC 1918 private (literal IP)
  207: #   169.254.0.0/16-- link-local; AWS/GCP/Azure metadata endpoint (literal IP)
  208: #   100.64.0.0/10 -- CGNAT / Tailscale shared space (literal IP)
  209: #
  210: # Hostname-based targets (e.g. http://internal.corp.example.com/) are allowed

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  211: # at this layer; block them with egress firewall rules instead.

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)

  240: # _safe_back_url($url) -> $url | undef
  241: #
  242: # Purpose: Sanitise a user-supplied back-link URL before it is passed to the
  243: #          template and rendered inside an href="" attribute.  Template Toolkit's
  244: #          | html filter encodes <>&" but does NOT block the javascript: or
  245: #          data: URI schemes, so a raw param('back') = "javascript:alert(1)"
  246: #          would be rendered verbatim inside the href and execute on click.
  247: #
  248: # Rule:    Only absolute http(s) URLs and root-relative paths (starting with /)
  249: #          are permitted.  Anything else (javascript:, data:, vbscript:, empty
  250: #          strings, undef) returns undef so the template renders no href.
  251: #
  252: sub _safe_back_url {
  253: 	my ($url) = @_;
  254: 	return undef unless defined $url && length($url);
  255: 	return $url if $url =~ m{\A(?:/(?!/)|https?://)}i;
  256: 	return undef;

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
  257: }

  348: # _scan_data_dir($self) -> \@table_list
  349: #
  350: # Purpose: Return an arrayref of { name, file } hashrefs for every supported
  351: #          data file in the configured data_dir.  Used by the index action; the
  352: #          join panel uses _scan_join_tables, which also lists internal tables.
  353: # Entry:   None.
  354: # Exit:    Returns [] when data_dir does not exist or contains no supported files.
  355: # Side Effects: Filesystem directory read.
  356: #
  357: # Optimisation: single-pass map replaces a two-pass grep+map chain, and calls
  358: # basename() only once per entry (the original chain called it twice: once in
  359: # grep to check the extension and again in map to extract the stem).
  360: # ->to_array avoids the extra flat-list expansion that ->each produced.
  361: sub _scan_data_dir :Protected ($self) {

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
  362: 	my $dir = $self->app->home->child($self->app->config->{data_dir} // 'data');

  364: 	return $dir->list->map(sub {
  365: 		my $base = $_->basename;
  366: 		return unless $base =~ $EXT_RE;
  367: 		(my $name = $base) =~ s/\.[^.]+\z//;
  368: 		{ name => $name, file => $base, path => $_->to_string }
  369: 	})->to_array;
  370: }

  513: # _get_columns($source, $records) -> @column_names
  514: #
  515: # Purpose: Return an ordered column list from a DataSource object.
  516: #          For CSV/TSV/PSV the DataSource stores the original file-header order.
  517: #          For SQLite/XML where no file-header order is available, the fallback
  518: #          is: id_column first (only if it actually appears in the data), then
  519: #          the remaining columns sorted alphabetically.
  520: # Entry:   $source is a DataSource; $records is an arrayref of hashrefs (may be empty).
  521: # Exit:    Returns a flat list of column-name strings (may be empty).
  522: #
  523: # Reduction: columns() is a pure accessor -- cache once to avoid a redundant
  524: # method dispatch on the second reference.
  525: sub _get_columns {
  526: 	my ($source, $records) = @_;
  527: 	my $cols = $source->columns;
  528: 	return @$cols if $cols;
  529: 	return () unless $records->[0];
  530: 	my %all = map { $_ => 1 } keys %{ $records->[0] };
  531: 	my $c   = $source->id_column;
  532: 	my $id  = ($c && $all{$c}) ? $c : (sort keys %all)[0];
  533: 	delete $all{$id};
  534: 	return ($id, sort keys %all);
  535: }

  537: # _apply_filter_spec($records, $spec) -> \@filtered_records
  538: #
  539: # Purpose: Apply one "col:op:val" filter to an arrayref of record hashrefs and
  540: #          return a new (possibly shorter) arrayref.  The original is not mutated.
  541: #          Value may contain colons: "col:op:val:with:colons" -- split limit 3
  542: #          puts everything after the second colon into $val.
  543: #          Unknown operators fall through to 1 (all rows pass) so new operators
  544: #          added in future do not break existing callers.
  545: # Entry:   $records is an arrayref of hashrefs; $spec is a colon-delimited string.
  546: # Exit:    Returns filtered arrayref (same reference if spec is invalid/unparseable).
  547: #
  548: # Domain Constraints (operator names):
  549: #   Operator names are CASE-SENSITIVE.  Only the exact lowercase forms below are
  550: #   recognised; an uppercase or mixed-case name (e.g. "EQ", "Lt") falls through
  551: #   to the default `1` branch and all rows pass unfiltered.
  552: #   Valid operators:
  553: #     String (value comparison is case-insensitive via lc()):
  554: #       eq, ne, contains, starts
  555: #     Numeric (Perl <, <=, >, >= coercion):
  556: #       lt (strict), le (inclusive), gt (strict), ge (inclusive)
  557: #     Unary (val is ignored):

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  558: #       empty (cell eq ''), notempty (cell ne '')
  559: #   Boundary rules for numeric operators:
  560: #     le/ge include the boundary value; lt/gt exclude it.
  561: #   contains/starts with empty val ('') match every record (index always 0).
  562: #
  563: # Reduction: split(/:/, $spec, 3) always produces at least one defined element,
  564: # so "defined $col" is structurally guaranteed true and is removed.
  565: # "$_->{$col} // ''" replaces the equivalent ternary form.
  566: #
  567: # Optimisation: $lval = lc($val) is computed once before the grep.  Without this
  568: # precompute, every case-insensitive operator (eq, ne, contains, starts) would
  569: # call lc($val) O(N) times for N records -- a pure O(N) waste since $val is
  570: # constant within a single filter application.
  571: sub _apply_filter_spec {
  572: 	my ($records, $spec) = @_;
  573: 	my ($col, $op, $val) = split /:/, $spec, 3;
  574: 	return $records unless length($col // '') && defined $op && length $op;
  575: 	$val //= '';
  576: 	my $lval = lc $val;	# precompute once; avoids O(N) redundant lc() inside grep
  577: 	return [grep {
  578: 		my $cell = $_->{$col} // '';
  579: 		$op eq 'eq'       ? lc($cell) eq $lval                :
  580: 		$op eq 'ne'       ? lc($cell) ne $lval                :
  581: 		$op eq 'contains' ? index(lc($cell), $lval) != -1     :
  582: 		$op eq 'starts'   ? index(lc($cell), $lval) == 0      :
  583: 		$op eq 'lt'       ? $cell <  $val                     :
  584: 		$op eq 'le'       ? $cell <= $val                     :
  585: 		$op eq 'gt'       ? $cell >  $val                     :
  586: 		$op eq 'ge'       ? $cell >= $val                     :
  587: 		$op eq 'empty'    ? $cell eq ''                        :
  588: 		$op eq 'notempty' ? $cell ne ''                        :
  589: 		1
  590: 	} @$records];
  591: }

  638: # _filter_specs_to_criteria( \@specs, $source ) -> ( \%criteria, \@residual_specs )
  639: #
  640: # Purpose: Map the f= filter vocabulary onto Database::Abstraction / Database::Join
  641: #          criteria hashrefs so the backend can apply pushable filters before
  642: #          paginating.  Specs that cannot be safely pushed are returned as
  643: #          @residual_specs for subsequent in-memory application.
  644: #
  645: # Pushdown is only performed when $source->dbi_source() confirms a SQL (SQLite)
  646: # backend.  For CSV (slurp path), in-memory, XLSX, BerkeleyDB and URL sources
  647: # everything is placed in @residual_specs so the existing _apply_filter_spec
  648: # Perl path handles them.
  649: #
  650: # Pushable mapping (SQLite only):
  651: #   eq / ne         -- LIKE / NOT LIKE without wildcards.  SQLite LIKE is
  652: #                      case-insensitive for ASCII, matching the existing Perl
  653: #                      lc() semantics.  Values containing % or _ are pushed to
  654: #                      residual to avoid LIKE metacharacter conflicts.
  655: #   contains        -- LIKE '%val%'.  Residual when value contains % or _.
  656: #   starts          -- LIKE 'val%'.   Residual when value contains % or _.
  657: #   lt / le / gt / ge -- SQL numeric comparison.  Residual when the value is
  658: #                      not a plain number (avoids column-affinity surprises
  659: #                      with currency or comma-formatted values).
  660: #   empty / notempty -- Always residual.  Must match both SQL NULL and the
  661: #                      literal empty string; cannot be expressed as a single
  662: #                      D::A criterion without an -or grouping.
  663: #
  664: # Column collision: if a column already has a pushable criterion, the second
  665: # spec for that column is placed in @residual_specs.  This keeps the AND logic
  666: # simple and safe; callers with multiple conditions on one column (e.g. a range)
  667: # still get correct results via the in-memory fallback.

  756: # Used by the Perl fold path of _grouped_totals so all four chart routes
  757: # share one extraction function.
  758: sub _numeric_cell {
  759: 	my $v = shift;
  760: 	return undef unless defined $v && length($v);
  761: 	my $is_acct_neg = ($v =~ /\A\s*\(/);
  762: 	(my $n = $v) =~ s/[^\d.\-]//g;
  763: 	$n = "-$n" if $is_acct_neg && $n =~ /\A\d/;
  764: 	return undef unless $n =~ /\A-?\d+(?:\.\d+)?\z/;
  765: 	return $n + 0;
  766: }

 1515: # _canonical_path($path) -> $string
 1516: #
 1517: # The absolute, symlink-resolved form of a path, for the data_dir guard.  Two
 1518: # spellings of one directory have to compare equal, or the guard can be walked

 1520: # points at, and a data_dir that is itself a symlink is still where the user
 1521: # keeps their data.  Cwd::realpath resolves the whole path when it exists; a

 1550: # _inside_dir($path, $dir) -> $bool
 1551: #
 1552: # True when $path names $dir itself or something below it.  abs2rel asks the
 1553: # file system what the relationship between the two is, so a textual prefix
 1554: # cannot make ".../data-archive" look like it is inside ".../data", and the
 1555: # comparison is made with the platform's separator rather than a hard-coded
 1556: # slash.
 1557: sub _inside_dir {
 1558: 	my ($path, $dir) = @_;
 1559: 	return 0 unless length $path && length $dir;
 1560: 	return 1 if $path eq $dir;
 1561: 
 1562: 	my $rel = eval { File::Spec->abs2rel($path, $dir) };
 1563: 	return 0 unless defined $rel && length $rel;
 1564: 	return 0 if File::Spec->file_name_is_absolute($rel);
 1565: 	return $rel eq '..' || $rel =~ m{\A\.\.[\\/]} ? 0 : 1;
 1566: }

 3206: # _decode_cell($s) -> $character_string

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 3207: #
 3208: # Purpose: Ensure $s is a Perl character string, not raw UTF-8 bytes.
 3209: #          URL-fetched data (via LWP::UserAgent::Cached + D::A HTML parsing)
 3210: #          arrives as byte strings without the UTF-8 flag set.  encode_json
 3211: #          then treats each byte as a Latin-1 code point and re-encodes it,
 3212: #          producing mojibake (e.g. an o-umlaut character displayed as two garbage bytes).  This helper
 3213: #          upgrades the flag in-place on a copy so the original hash key is
 3214: #          unchanged but the value passed to HTML::D3 is a proper character.
 3215: # Entry:   $s -- any defined scalar (undef returned as-is).
 3216: # Exit:    The same string value, but with the UTF-8 flag set if it was absent.
 3217: sub _decode_cell {
 3218: 	my $s = shift;
 3219: 	return $s unless defined $s;
 3220: 	utf8::decode($s) unless utf8::is_utf8($s);
 3221: 	$s;
 3222: }

 3224: # _serialize_csv($records, \@columns) -> $utf8_string
 3225: #
 3226: # Purpose: Build a complete RFC 4180 CSV document (header + data rows) from
 3227: #          $records.  Shared by _render_csv (HTTP download) and the csv branch
 3228: #          of export_write (filesystem write) to eliminate duplicated logic.
 3229: # Entry:   $records is an arrayref of hashrefs; $columns is an arrayref of names.
 3230: # Exit:    Returns a UTF-8 Perl string ending with CRLF.
 3231: #
 3232: # Optimisation: rows are collected in @lines and joined in one operation.
 3233: # The previous pattern ($out .= _csv_row(...) per row) triggers O(N) string
 3234: # reallocations; joining a pre-built array is a single O(total_bytes) allocation.
 3235: # For exports of thousands of rows the difference is measurable.
 3236: #
 3237: # XS note: for very large exports, replacing _csv_row with Text::CSV_XS
 3238: # (available from CPAN) would further reduce serialisation time -- its C-level
 3239: # implementation is roughly 5-10x faster than the pure-Perl version here.
 3240: sub _serialize_csv {
●3241 → 3243 → 3246 3241: 	my ($records, $columns) = @_;
 3242: 	my @lines = _csv_row(@$columns);
 3243: 	for my $row (@$records) {
 3244: 		push @lines, _csv_row(map { $row->{$_} } @$columns);
 3245: 	}
 3246: 	return encode('UTF-8', join('', @lines));
 3247: }
 3248:

 3367: =head2 view
 3368: 
 3369: C<GET /view/:table> -- Open and display the chosen table from C<data_dir>.
 3370: 
 3371: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3372: 
 3373: =head4 INPUT
 3374: 
 3375:   :table   string   Table name (alphanumeric + underscore).  Returns 404 on
 3376:                     other characters.
 3377:   ?f=      string   (repeatable) Filter spec: "col:op:val".
 3378: 
 3379: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS: :table
 3380: 
 3381: C<:table> is validated against C<\A[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*\z> before C<open_table>
 3382: is ever called.  The first character must be a letter (A-Z, a-z) or an
 3383: underscore; subsequent characters may also include digits (0-9).
 3384: 
 3385: =over 4
 3386: 
 3387: =item Valid partition
 3388: 
 3389: C<sales> (letters only), C<_temp> (underscore-start), C<report_2024>
 3390: (mixed).  A name that is valid but has no backing file returns 200 with
 3391: an error message -- NOT 404.
 3392: 
 3393: =item Invalid partition
 3394: 
 3395: C<1sales> (digit-start, 404), C<my.data> (dot, 404), C<my-data>
 3396: (hyphen, 404), non-ASCII characters (404).
 3397: 
 3398: =item Boundary values
 3399: 
 3400: C<a> (single letter, valid), C<_> (single underscore, valid), C<1>
 3401: (single digit, 404), C<a1> (letter then digit, valid), C<1a> (digit
 3402: then letter, 404).
 3403:

 3587: 			unshift @crumbs, { name => $name, href => '/browse?path=' . url_escape($f->to_string) };
 3588: 			my $parent = $f->dirname;
 3589: 			last if $parent->to_string eq $f->to_string;
 3590: 			$f = $parent;
 3591: 		}
 3592: 	}
 3593: 
 3594: 	my $parent      = $dir->dirname;
 3595: 	my $parent_href = $parent->to_string ne $dir->to_string
 3596: 		? '/browse?path=' . url_escape($parent->to_string)
 3597: 		: undef;
 3598:

 3612: =head2 open_file
 3613: 
 3614: C<GET /open> -- Open a supported data file from any absolute filesystem path.
 3615: 
 3616: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3617: 
 3618: =head4 INPUT
 3619: 
 3620:   ?path=   string   Absolute path to the data file.  Returns 404 when missing,
 3621:                     not a regular file, or the extension is not in SUPPORTED_EXT.
 3622:   ?f=      string   (repeatable) Filter spec: "col:op:val".
 3623: 
 3624: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS: ?path=
 3625: 
 3626: =over 4
 3627: 
 3628: =item Extension filter (C<EXT_RE>)
 3629: 
 3630: The basename must match C<\.(?:csv|db|sql|xml|tsv|psv)\z> (case-insensitive).
 3631: The C<\z> anchor (absolute end-of-string) means a URL-encoded trailing
 3632: newline (e.g. C<file.csv%0A> decoded to C<file.csv\n>) does NOT pass --
 3633: the C<\n> falls after the C<\z> boundary and the extension check fails.
 3634: 
 3635: =item Valid partition
 3636: 
 3637: C</data/sales.csv> (lowercase extension), C</tmp/REPORT.CSV> (uppercase
 3638: extension, /i matches), any C<.db>, C<.sql>, C<.xml>, C<.tsv>, C<.psv> regular
 3639: file.
 3640: 
 3641: =item Invalid partition
 3642: 
 3643: Absent C<?path=> (404), non-existent file (404), directory instead of
 3644: file (404), unsupported extension such as C<.txt> (404), empty string
 3645: (404), path containing a C<%0A> (newline) suffix (404).
 3646: 
 3647: =item Double-extension filenames
 3648: 
 3649: A file like C<file.php.csv> passes C<EXT_RE> (last segment is C<.csv>)
 3650: but produces table stem C<file.php> which fails C<DataSource>'s
 3651: C<TABLE_NAME_RE>.  The C<open_table> call is inside C<eval>, so the
 3652: croak is caught and the action returns 200 with a friendly error, not

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 3653: a 500.

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 3654: 
 3655: =back
 3656: 
 3657: =head4 OUTPUT
 3658: 
 3659: On success renders C<dashboard.html.tt> with C<back_url> pointing to the
 3660: directory's C</browse> page, and C<file_path> set to the resolved absolute
 3661: path (used by the template to record the file in C<localStorage>).
 3662: On error re-renders C<home.html.tt> with C<error>, C<back_url>, C<back_label>.
 3663: 
 3664: =head3 MESSAGES
 3665:

 4123: =head2 join_tables
 4124: 
 4125: C<GET /join> -- Perform one or more left joins and render the merged table.
 4126: 
 4127: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 4128: 
 4129: =head4 INPUT
 4130: 
 4131:   ?l=    string   Required. Left table spec: "table:name", "table:file/table"
 4132:                   or "path:/abs/path".  Returns 404 if unresolvable.
 4133:   ?j=    string   (repeatable) Join step: "<right-spec>|<left-key>|<right-key>".
 4134:                   Invalid or non-existent steps are silently skipped.
 4135:   ?f=    string   (repeatable) Result filter: "col:op:val".
 4136: 
 4137: =head4 OUTPUT
 4138: 
 4139: Renders C<dashboard.html.tt> with the merged/filtered result set.
 4140: 
 4141: =head3 MESSAGES
 4142: 
 4143:   error_table_open   -- Left table fetch threw (re-renders home with error).
 4144: 
 4145: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 4146: 
 4147:   join_tables == lambda self .
 4148:     let left = open_spec(param('l')) in
 4149:     pre  left /= undef
 4150:     let joined  = fold(_left_join, left, param_list('j')) in
 4151:     let filtered = fold(_apply_filter_spec, joined, param_list('f')) in
 4152:     post render(dashboard, records: filtered)
 4153: 
 4154: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 4155: 
 4156:   1. Parse and open left table spec; 404 on failure.
 4157:   2. For each j= param (in order):
 4158:        a. Parse right-spec, left-key, right-key from "|"-split.
 4159:        b. Verify left-key exists in current column list.
 4160:        c. Open right table; skip step on any error.
 4161:        d. Verify right-key exists in right column list.
 4162:        e. Call _left_join; update records and columns.
 4163:   3. Apply all f= filters via _apply_filter_spec.
 4164:   4. Build stable table key for localStorage ("join:left:right1:...").
 4165:   5. Render dashboard.
 4166: 
 4167: =head3 EXAMPLE
 4168: 
 4169:   GET /join?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name
 4170:     -> merged left join of sales and products on product/name columns
 4171: 

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 4172: =cut

 4291: unified view.
 4292: 
 4293: Unlike C</join> (which appends columns from a matching row in a second table),
 4294: C</combine> appends the I<rows> from a second table beneath the rows of the
 4295: first.  All columns from all sources appear as headers; where a row has no
 4296: value for a particular column (because that column did not exist in its source
 4297: file) the cell is left blank.
 4298: 
 4299: This is equivalent to a SQL C<UNION ALL> across heterogeneous schemas.

 4522: =head2 export_write
 4523: 
 4524: C<POST /export> -- Write the current logical view to a chosen filesystem path.
 4525: 
 4526: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 4527: 
 4528: =head4 INPUT
 4529: 
 4530:   l=         string   (required) Left table spec.
 4531:   j=         string   (repeatable) Join steps.
 4532:   f=         string   (repeatable) Filter specs.
 4533:   dir=       string   Target directory (must exist; resolved via realpath).
 4534:   filename=  string   Output filename including extension.  Extension determines
 4535:                       format: C<.csv> -> RFC 4180 CSV; C<.sql> -> SQLite;
 4536:                       C<.json> -> JSON array of row objects.
 4537: 
 4538: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS: filename=
 4539: 
 4540: The extension check uses C</\.csv\z/i> (CSV), C</\.sql\z/i> (SQLite), or
 4541: C</\.json\z/i> (JSON): the C</i> flag makes matching case-insensitive, so
 4542: C<.CSV>, C<.SQL>, and C<.JSON> are accepted alongside lowercase forms.
 4543: Everything else returns 415.
 4544: 
 4545: Path separator characters (C</> and C<\>) in C<filename> are stripped
 4546: first via C<m{([^/\\]+)\z}> -- only the basename is kept, preventing
 4547: directory traversal.
 4548: 
 4549: =over 4
 4550: 
 4551: =item Valid partitions
 4552: 
 4553: C<report.csv>, C<report.sql>, C<report.json>, C<REPORT.CSV>, C<report.SQL>.
 4554: A single-character stem (C<a.csv>) is also valid.
 4555: 
 4556: =item Invalid partitions (415)
 4557: 
 4558: C<report.txt>, C<report> (no extension), empty string.
 4559: 
 4560: =back
 4561: 
 4562: =head4 OUTPUT
 4563: 
 4564:   200 application/json   { "saved": "/abs/path/to/file" }

 4682: sub dirs_api ($self) {
 4683: 	my $raw = $self->param('path') // $ENV{HOME} // '/';
 4684: 	my $dir = eval { Mojo::File->new($raw)->realpath };
 4685: 	return $self->render(json => { error => 'Not a directory' }, status => 404)
 4686: 		unless defined $dir && -d $dir;
 4687: 
 4688: 	my $listing = $self->_list_dir($dir, 0);
 4689: 	my $parent  = $dir->dirname;
 4690: 
 4691: 	$self->render(json => {
 4692: 		path   => $dir->to_string,
 4693: 		parent => ($parent->to_string ne $dir->to_string ? $parent->to_string : undef),
 4694: 		dirs   => $listing->{dirs},
 4695: 	});

 4698: =head2 stat_api
 4699: 
 4700: C<GET /api/stat> -- Return filesystem metadata for a file path.
 4701: 
 4702: Used by the home page tooltip to show modification time and size for recently
 4703: opened files without a page reload.
 4704: 
 4705: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 4706: 
 4707: =head4 INPUT
 4708: 
 4709:   ?path=   string   Absolute path to query.  Returns HTTP 400 when absent.
 4710: 
 4711: =head4 OUTPUT
 4712: 
 4713:   200 application/json   (file exists)
 4714:     { "exists": true, "path": "/resolved/path", "mtime": 1700000000, "size": 4096 }
 4715: 
 4716:   200 application/json   (file does not exist)
 4717:     { "exists": false, "path": "/original/path" }
 4718: 
 4719:   400 application/json   { "error": "\"path\" parameter is required" }
 4720: 
 4721: C<mtime> is Unix epoch seconds.  C<size> is bytes.  A missing/unresolvable
 4722: path returns HTTP 200 with C<exists: false> (not 404) so the UI can
 4723: distinguish "file was deleted" from a request error.
 4724: 
 4725: =head3 MESSAGES
 4726: 
 4727:   error_path_required   -- the "path" query parameter was not supplied.
 4728: 
 4729: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 4730: 
 4731:   stat_api == lambda self .
 4732:     let path = param('path') in
 4733:     pre  path /= undef
 4734:     let file = realpath(path) in
 4735:     post if is_file(file) then
 4736:            render_json({ exists: true, mtime: mtime(file), size: size(file) })
 4737:          else render_json({ exists: false })
 4738: 
 4739: =head3 EXAMPLE
 4740: 
 4741:   GET /api/stat?path=/data/sales.csv
 4742:     -> { "exists": true, "path": "/data/sales.csv", "mtime": 1700000000, "size": 1234 }
 4743: 
 4744:   GET /api/stat?path=/deleted.csv
 4745:     -> { "exists": false, "path": "/deleted.csv" }
 4746: 
 4747: =cut
 4748:

 4795: =head2 upload_file
 4796: 
 4797: C<POST /upload> -- Accept a drag-and-dropped data file and return a redirect URL.
 4798: 
 4799: The file is saved under its original filename in a managed subdirectory of
 4800: C<< <app_home>/.uploads/ >>.  The subdirectory name is randomised so that
 4801: concurrent uploads of files with the same name do not collide.

 4810: 
 4811: =head4 OUTPUT
 4812: 
 4813:   200 application/json   { "url": "/open?path=/abs/path/file.csv", "path": "/abs/path/file.csv" }
 4814:   400 application/json   { "error": "No file received" }
 4815:   413 application/json   { "error": "File too large to upload (limit: 50 MB)" }
 4816:   415 application/json   { "error": "Unsupported file type. Accepted: ..." }
 4817:   500 application/json   { "error": "Could not save uploaded file" }
 4818: 
 4819: =head3 MESSAGES
 4820: 
 4821:   error_upload_none       -- no file was received in the multipart upload.
 4822:   error_upload_ext        -- the file's extension is not in the supported list.
 4823:   error_upload_too_large  -- the upload body exceeded MAX_UPLOAD_MIB (50 MiB).
 4824:   error_upload_save       -- move_to failed or the destination file was not
 4825:                              created (I/O error, full disk, permission error).
 4826: 
 4827: =head3 IMPLEMENTATION NOTES
 4828: 
 4829: The C<move_to> call is wrapped with the C<eval { ...; 1 }> idiom (not
 4830: C<eval {}> + C<if ($@)>).  Mojolicious dispatch and DESTROY-phase evals can
 4831: leave C<$@> set before the action runs; checking C<if ($@)> after a new eval
 4832: may fire falsely on that stale value.  C<eval { move_to(); 1 }> returns 1 on
 4833: success and C<undef> on exception, so the result is independent of any prior
 4834: C<$@> value.
 4835: 
 4836: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 4837: 
 4838:   upload_file == lambda self .
 4839:     let upload = req.upload('file') in
 4840:     pre  upload /= undef /\ basename(upload.filename) =~ EXT_RE
 4841:          /\ NOT req->is_limit_exceeded
 4842:          /\ upload.size <= MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES
 4843:     let dest = home/.uploads/<random>/<filename> in
 4844:     let ok = eval { upload.move_to(dest); 1 } in
 4845:     pre  ok /\ -f dest
 4846:     post render_json({ url: '/open?path=' ++ url_escape(dest), path: dest })
 4847: 
 4848: =head3 EXAMPLE
 4849: 
 4850:   POST /upload  (multipart: file=@sales.csv)
 4851:     -> { "url": "/open?path=%2F...%2Fsales.csv", "path": "/.../.uploads/abc123/sales.csv" }
 4852: 
 4853: =cut
 4854:

 5614: =head2 folder_view
 5615: 
 5616: C<GET /folder> -- Browse a data table grouped by the letter-prefix hierarchy
 5617: of an order-number column (e.g. A10, CT4, B3).
 5618: 
 5619: The top level displays one folder icon per initial letter group (A, B, C ...).
 5620: Clicking a folder with sub-prefixes (e.g. C contains CA and CB items) shows
 5621: the sub-folders; clicking a leaf prefix shows the actual data rows sorted
 5622: numerically by the embedded digit sequence.
 5623: 
 5624: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 5625: 
 5626:   col=<col>      Column whose values contain order codes (optional; auto-
 5627:                  detected when absent by scanning the first 10 rows for
 5628:                  values matching C<[A-Z]{1,4}\d+[A-Z]?>)
 5629:   prefix=<str>   Current drill-down prefix, e.g. "CA" (optional; top-level

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 5630:                  when absent)
 5631:   back=<url>     URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link (optional;
 5632:                  derived from C<l=> when absent)
 5633: 
 5634: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 5635: 
 5636: =head4 INPUT
 5637: 
 5638:   l       spec     Required.  Left table spec (table:name or path:/abs/path).
 5639:   col     string   Optional.  Order-code column name.
 5640:   prefix  string   Optional.  Current letter prefix; max length MAX_PREFIX_LEN.
 5641:   back    url      Optional.  Safe back-link URL (sanitised by _safe_back_url).
 5642: 
 5643: =head4 OUTPUT
 5644: 
 5645:   200 text/html    Rendered folder page with folder grid and/or item table.
 5646:   400 text/plain   "Missing l= parameter" when l= is absent.
 5647:   400 text/plain   "Prefix too long" when prefix exceeds MAX_PREFIX_LEN (20).
 5648:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source" when the data source is unavailable.
 5649: 
 5650: =head3 MESSAGES
 5651: 
 5652: None -- all failure paths render plain text with an HTTP status code.
 5653: 
 5654: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 5655: 
 5656:   folder_view == lambda self .
 5657:     pre length(left_spec) > 0 /\ length(prefix) <= MAX_PREFIX_LEN

 7127: 	# Server-side binning: compute equal-width bins over the data range.
 7128: 	# Values equal to $max land in the highest bin (no overflow bar).
 7129: 	# Uses the same bin-boundary logic as Calc.pm bin() to stay consistent with
 7130: 	# ?c=band:bin(col,width) computed columns on the same data.

 7132: 	my $min_v = min(@values);
 7133: 	my $max_v = max(@values);
 7134: 	my @bin_data;
 7135: 	if ($min_v == $max_v) {
 7136: 		# All values identical: one bin.
 7137: 		push @bin_data, { label => "$min_v", count => scalar @values, lo => $min_v, hi => $min_v };
 7138: 	} else {
 7139: 		my $raw_w = ($max_v - $min_v) / $bins;
 7140: 		# Round up to a "nice" width using 10 significant digits (same guard as Calc bin()).
 7141: 		my $width = sprintf('%.10g', $raw_w) + 0;
 7142: 		$width = $raw_w if $width <= 0;  # safety: never produce zero width
 7143: 		my $fmt = ($width == int($width)) ? '%d' : '%g';
 7144: 
 7145: 		# Build bin boundaries.
 7146: 		my @bin_counts;
 7147: 		my @bin_los;
 7148: 		for my $i (0 .. $bins - 1) {
 7149: 			push @bin_los, $min_v + $i * $width;
 7150: 			push @bin_counts, 0;
 7151: 		}
 7152: 
 7153: 		# Assign each value to a bin.
 7154: 		for my $v (@values) {
 7155: 			my $idx = floor(sprintf('%.10g', ($v - $min_v) / $width) + 0);
 7156: 			$idx = $bins - 1 if $idx >= $bins;  # max value -> last bin
 7157: 			$idx = 0         if $idx < 0;
 7158: 			$bin_counts[$idx]++;
 7159: 		}
 7160: 
 7161: 		for my $i (0 .. $bins - 1) {
 7162: 			next unless $bin_counts[$i];
 7163: 			my $lo  = $bin_los[$i];
 7164: 			my $hi  = $lo + $width;
 7165: 			my $lbl = sprintf("[${fmt},${fmt})", $lo, $hi);
 7166: 			push @bin_data, { label => $lbl, count => $bin_counts[$i], lo => $lo, hi => $hi };
 7167: 		}
 7168: 	}

 7208: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 7209: # Map  (GET /map)
 7210: #
 7211: # Phase 6.1/6.2: one route, one template, two ways for a row to get a
 7212: # position.  With lat= and lon= the columns already hold coordinates; with
 7213: # loc=<column> every distinct value of that column is geocoded once, by
 7214: # Database::BI::Model::Geocode.  Answers are remembered in one shared cache in
 7215: # the model store, keyed by the place rather than by the file or column that
 7216: # named it, so a second visit asks nothing and a second dataset naming the same
 7217: # place asks nothing either.  Everything before that - the pipeline, calculated
 7218: # fields and f= filters - is the same as every other visual, so a map shows the
 7219: # filtered, calculated view and not the raw table.
 7220: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 7221: 
 7222: # _map_data($self, \@records, \@columns, \%encode, \%options, $spec_key, $id_suffix)
 7223: #     -> \%result
 7224: #
 7225: # Resolve a map's markers and draw its HTML.  The map page and a map card on a
 7226: # canvas both come through here: a map spec names either loc=<column> (places
 7227: # to geocode) or lat=/lon= (coordinates already in the data), and everything
 7228: # else - the marker cap, popups, the geocode cache and HTML::OSM - is the same
 7229: # in both places.  %result has { map_body, map_head, title, messages, ... } on success
 7230: # or { error, status } when the spec cannot be drawn.  $id_suffix makes the
 7231: # Leaflet element ids unique when several maps share one page; undef leaves the
 7232: # map page's own ids alone.

 7506: # _replace_map_tile_url($body, $url) -> $body
 7507: #
 7508: # Replace the tile layer URL in a body rendered by HTML::OSM releases that
 7509: # predate tile_url.  The URL is escaped for the single-quoted JavaScript
 7510: # literal the renderer emits, so a quote, backslash or newline in the config
 7511: # cannot end the string or the enclosing <script>.
 7512: sub _replace_map_tile_url {
 7513: 	my ($body, $url) = @_;
 7514: 	return $body unless defined($body) && defined($url) && length($url);
 7515: 
 7516: 	my $escaped = $url;
 7517: 	$escaped =~ s/\\/\\\\/g;
 7518: 	$escaped =~ s/'/\\'/g;
 7519: 	$escaped =~ s/\r?\n/\\n/g;
 7520: 	$escaped =~ s|</|<\\/|g;
 7521: 
 7522: 	$body =~ s{\Q$DEFAULT_TILE_URL\E}{$escaped}g;
 7523: 	return $body;
 7524: }
 7525: 
 7526: # _canvas_map_html($head, $body, $id_suffix) -> ($head, $body)
 7527: #
 7528: # HTML::OSM names the map element "map" and its controls "search-box",
 7529: # "reset-button" and "clear-search-button".  Those names are fine for one map
 7530: # on a page and collide as soon as a canvas has two, so a card suffixes them and
 7531: # wraps the generated script in a function: the script's map and searchMarkers
 7532: # variables become local to that card instead of overwriting the previous
 7533: # map's.  The head is the same Leaflet stylesheet and script for every map, so
 7534: # only the first head is passed to the layout.
 7535: sub _canvas_map_html {
●[NOT COVERED] 7536 → 7548 → 7554 7536: 	my ($head, $body, $id_suffix) = @_;
 7537: 
 7538: 	my $suffix = $id_suffix // 'map';
 7539: 	$suffix =~ s/[^A-Za-z0-9_-]//g;
 7540: 	$suffix = 'map' unless length $suffix;
 7541: 
 7542: 	my %ids = (
 7543: 		'map'                 => "map-$suffix",
 7544: 		'search-box'          => "search-box-$suffix",
 7545: 		'reset-button'        => "reset-button-$suffix",
 7546: 		'clear-search-button' => "clear-search-button-$suffix",
 7547: 	);
 7548: 	for my $old (sort keys %ids) {
 7549: 		my $new = $ids{$old};
 7550: 		$body =~ s/id="\Q$old\E"/id="$new"/g;
 7551: 		$body =~ s{getElementById\('\Q$old\E'\)}{getElementById('$new')}g;
 7552: 		$body =~ s{L\.map\('\Q$old\E'\)}{L.map('$new')}g;
 7553: 	}
 7554: 	$body =~ s{<div id="$ids{map}"></div>}{<div id="$ids{map}" class="canvas-map"></div>};
 7555: 
 7556: 	# The card owns the map's size, so the page-wide #map rule is noise here.
 7557: 	$head =~ s{<style>.*?</style>}{}s;
 7558: 
 7559: 	# One map per card, one local scope per map.  The replacement uses !
 7560: 	# as its delimiter because the wrapper itself contains braces.
 7561: 	$body =~ s!<script>\s*!<script>\n(function () {\n!;
 7562: 	$body =~ s!</script>!})();\n</script>!;
 7563: 
 7564: 	return ($head, $body);
 7565: }

 7644: # _map_popup($heading, $rows, \@extras) -> $html
 7645: #
 7646: # The popup for one marker: what it is, how many rows it stands for, and the
 7647: # columns the request asked for.  Leaflet treats a popup string as HTML, and a
 7648: # place name out of a user's file is not markup, so every value is escaped here.
 7649: sub _map_popup {
 7650: 	my ($heading, $rows, $extras) = @_;
 7651: 
 7652: 	my @lines = '<strong>' . xml_escape($heading // '') . '</strong>';
 7653: 	push @lines, $rows . ($rows == 1 ? ' row' : ' rows');
 7654: 	push @lines, map { xml_escape($_->[0]) . ': ' . xml_escape($_->[1]) }
 7655: 		grep { defined($_->[1]) && length($_->[1]) } @{ $extras // [] };
 7656: 
 7657: 	return join('<br>', @lines);
 7658: }
 7659: 
 7660: # _map_limit($value, $default) -> $integer
 7661: #
 7662: # A positive integer from a config value or request parameter, or the default.
 7663: # An absent, empty or nonsensical map setting is an operator's typo, not a data
 7664: # error, so the page falls back instead of refusing.
 7665: sub _map_limit {
 7666: 	my ($value, $default) = @_;
 7667: 
 7668: 	return $default unless defined($value) && ($value =~ /\A\d+\z/) && ($value > 0);
 7669: 
 7670: 	return $value + 0;
 7671: }

 7673: # _iso8601_utc() -> $string
 7674: #
 7675: # When a geocode cache was built, in the form the model store records.
 7676: sub _iso8601_utc {
 7677: 	require POSIX;
 7678: 
 7679: 	return POSIX::strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', gmtime());
 7680: }

 7685: 
 7686: =head1 NAME
 7687: 
 7688: Database::BI::Controller::Dashboard - Home picker, filesystem browser, table
 7689: viewer, left-join engine, result filter, export, and file upload
 7690: 
 7691: =head1 SYNOPSIS
 7692: 
 7693: All routes in C<Database::BI> are handled by this controller.  You do not
 7694: call its methods directly -- Mojolicious dispatches HTTP requests to them
 7695: automatically.  The examples below show browser URLs and their curl
 7696: equivalents.
 7697: 
 7698: B<Open the home page and see all tables in the data directory:>
 7699: 
 7700:   # Browser
 7701:   http://localhost:3000/
 7702: 
 7703:   # curl
 7704:   curl http://localhost:3000/
 7705: 
 7706: B<View a single table (file: data/sales.csv):>
 7707: 
 7708:   # Browser
 7709:   http://localhost:3000/view/sales
 7710: 
 7711:   # curl
 7712:   curl http://localhost:3000/view/sales
 7713: 
 7714: B<Filter rows -- show only rows where region equals "North":>
 7715: 
 7716:   # Browser (add ?f=col:op:val to any view URL)
 7717:   http://localhost:3000/view/sales?f=region:eq:North
 7718: 
 7719:   # Multiple filters -- "North" AND amount greater than 100:
 7720:   http://localhost:3000/view/sales?f=region:eq:North&f=amount:gt:100
 7721: 
 7722:   # curl
 7723:   curl 'http://localhost:3000/view/sales?f=region:eq:North'
 7724: 
 7725: B<Join two tables -- left-join sales with products on the product column:>
 7726: 
 7727:   # Browser
 7728:   http://localhost:3000/join?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name
 7729: 
 7730:   # The j= parameter is: right-table-spec | left-key | right-key
 7731:   # You can chain multiple joins:
 7732:   http://localhost:3000/join?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name&j=table:regions|region|id
 7733: 
 7734: B<Open a file anywhere on the filesystem (not just in data/):>
 7735: 
 7736:   http://localhost:3000/open?path=/home/user/reports/q3.csv
 7737: 
 7738: B<Download the current view as a CSV file:>
 7739: 
 7740:   # Uses the same l=, j=, f= parameters as /join
 7741:   http://localhost:3000/export?l=table:sales&format=csv
 7742: 
 7743:   # Download as a SQLite database file instead:
 7744:   http://localhost:3000/export?l=table:sales&format=sqlite
 7745: 
 7746:   # Download a filtered + joined result:
 7747:   http://localhost:3000/export?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name&f=region:eq:North&format=csv
 7748: 
 7749: B<Save the current view to a file on the server (instead of downloading):>
 7750: 
 7751:   # POST with form fields; format is inferred from the filename extension
 7752:   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/export \
 7753:        -F l=table:sales \
 7754:        -F dir=/home/user/exports \
 7755:        -F filename=report.csv
 7756: 
 7757:   # Save as SQLite:
 7758:   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/export \
 7759:        -F l=table:sales \
 7760:        -F dir=/home/user/exports \
 7761:        -F filename=report.sql
 7762: 
 7763: B<Get the column list for a table (used by the join UI):>
 7764: 
 7765:   curl http://localhost:3000/api/columns?table=sales
 7766:   # Returns: {"columns":["product","region","amount","date"]}
 7767: 
 7768: B<Check when a file was last modified (used by the tooltip on the home page):>
 7769: 
 7770:   curl 'http://localhost:3000/api/stat?path=/data/sales.csv'
 7771:   # Returns: {"exists":true,"path":"/data/sales.csv","mtime":1700000000,"size":1234}
 7772: 
 7773: B<Browse the filesystem to find a data file:>
 7774: 
 7775:   http://localhost:3000/browse
 7776:   http://localhost:3000/browse?path=/home/user/data
 7777: 
 7778: B<Upload a data file by dropping it onto the page (multipart form POST):>
 7779: 
 7780:   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/upload \
 7781:        -F file=@/home/user/data/sales.csv
 7782:   # Returns: {"url":"/open?path=/.../.uploads/.../sales.csv","path":"/.../.uploads/.../sales.csv"}
 7783: 
 7784: =head2 graph_view
 7785: 
 7786: C<GET /graph> -- Render a D3.js v7 line chart from the current data pipeline.
 7787: 
 7788: Accepts the same pipeline parameters as L</join_tables> (C<l=>, C<j=>,
 7789: C<c=>, C<f=>, C<d=>) plus:
 7790: 
 7791: =over 4
 7792: 
 7793: =item C<x=E<lt>colE<gt>>
 7794: 
 7795: Column name to use as the X axis (required).  Any data type is accepted;
 7796: values are displayed as categorical labels.
 7797: 
 7798: =item C<y=E<lt>colE<gt>>
 7799: 
 7800: Column name to use as the Y axis (required).  Non-numeric characters
 7801: (currency symbols, commas, etc.) are stripped before comparison; rows
 7802: where the value is still non-numeric after stripping are silently skipped.
 7803: 
 7804: =item C<back=E<lt>urlE<gt>>
 7805: 
 7806: URL for the "Back to table" link rendered in the graph toolbar.  Defaults
 7807: to C</> when absent.  The dashboard JS sets this to the current page URL
 7808: before navigating.
 7809: 
 7810: =back
 7811: 
 7812: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7813: 
 7814: =head4 INPUT
 7815: 
 7816:   l=<spec>     string   Left-table spec (required).  Same syntax as /join.
 7817:   x=<col>      string   X-axis column name (required).
 7818:   y=<col>      string   Y-axis column name (required; must be numeric).
 7819:   back=<url>   string   Back-link URL (optional; default "/").
 7820:   j=, c=, f=, d=        Pipeline params -- see L</join_tables>.
 7821: 
 7822: =head4 OUTPUT
 7823: 
 7824:   200 text/html   TT-rendered graph page.  The HTML::D3 snippet embeds a
 7825:                   C<const data = [...]> JSON array where each element has:
 7826:                     { label: <x-value>, value: <y-value>,
 7827:                       extra: { <col>: <val>, ... } }
 7828:                   C<extra> contains every column not used as X or Y, so
 7829:                   the hover tooltip shows the complete row for each point.
 7830:                   The chart supports brush-to-zoom; a "Reset zoom" button
 7831:                   appears after a brush selection.  The toolbar shows the
 7832:                   count of plotted data points.
 7833: 
 7834:   400 text/plain  C<x=> or C<y=> absent, or named column not found.
 7835:   404 text/plain  Data source could not be opened.
 7836: 
 7837: =head3 MESSAGES
 7838: 
 7839:   Missing x or y column parameter          C<x=> or C<y=> query param absent.
 7840:   Column not found: <name>                  Named column absent from result set.
 7841:   Could not open data source                Left-table spec unresolvable or 404.
 7842:   No plottable data: no rows have valid ... All rows lack a numeric Y value.
 7843: 
 7844: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7845: 
 7846:   GET /graph?l=table:sales&x=product&y=amount
 7847: 
 7848: Renders a line chart of C<amount> (Y) against C<product> (X).  Mousing over
 7849: a dot shows a tooltip with the product name, the amount, and every other
 7850: column in that row (e.g. C<region>, C<date>).
 7851: 
 7852: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 7853: 
 7854:   graph_view : Controller Ã— Params â†’ HTML | Error
 7855: 
 7856:   let pipeline = run_export_pipeline(l, j, c, f, d)
 7857:   let pairs    = [ [row[x], num(row[y]), row \ {x,y}]
 7858:                    | row âˆˆ pipeline.records,  num(row[y]) â‰  âŠ¥ ]
 7859: 
 7860:   pre  x âˆˆ params âˆ§ y âˆˆ params           else 400
 7861:   pre  x âˆˆ pipeline.cols                  else 400
 7862:   pre  y âˆˆ pipeline.cols                  else 400
 7863:   pre  pairs â‰  []                          else 200 "No plottable data"
 7864:   post HTML::D3->render_line_chart_snippet(pairs) embedded in TT layout
 7865: 
 7866: =cut
 7867: 
 7868: =head2 pie_view
 7869: 
 7870: C<GET /pie> -- Render a D3.js v7 animated pie chart from the current data
 7871: pipeline.
 7872: 
 7873: Each row's category value (C<cat=>) is used as a slice label; the numeric
 7874: value column (C<val=>) is summed per category.  Accounting-notation
 7875: negatives such as C<(1,234.56)> are handled automatically.  Slices are
 7876: sorted by total descending; when there are more than 12 distinct categories
 7877: the smallest ones are collapsed into a single "Other" slice.
 7878: 
 7879: Clicking any slice or legend entry navigates back to the table view with
 7880: an C<f=cat:eq:value> filter applied automatically, so the user can drill
 7881: into the data behind a slice without leaving the application.
 7882: 
 7883: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7884: 
 7885: =head4 INPUT
 7886: 
 7887:   l=<spec>    string   Left-table spec (required).  Same syntax as /join.
 7888:   cat=<col>   string   Category column name (required; used as slice labels).
 7889:   val=<col>   string   Numeric value column name (required; values are summed).
 7890:   donut=1     flag     Render as a donut chart with a hole in the centre (optional).
 7891:   back=<url>  string   URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link (optional; default "/").
 7892:   f=<spec>    string   Result filter (repeatable; same syntax as /join).
 7893: 
 7894: =head4 OUTPUT
 7895: 
 7896:   200 text/html   TT-rendered pie chart page containing:
 7897:                   - Two-level breadcrumb (Home > Back to table).
 7898:                   - Export buttons for SVG and PNG (legend is composited in).
 7899:                   - An animated D3.js pie or donut chart with a colour-coded
 7900:                     legend.  Each slice and legend entry is clickable and
 7901:                     navigates to the filtered table view.
 7902:                   - Slice count (e.g. "7 slices").
 7903: 
 7904:   200 text/plain  "No plottable data" when every row has a non-numeric or
 7905:                   missing value column (HTTP 200, not 4xx, so the page can
 7906:                   render a friendly message).
 7907: 
 7908:   400 text/plain  C<cat=> or C<val=> absent, or named column not found.
 7909:   404 text/plain  Data source could not be opened.
 7910: 
 7911: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS
 7912: 
 7913:   cat=    Any column name that exists in the result set.  Cell values are
 7914:           used verbatim as slice labels.  Empty-string cells are skipped.
 7915: 
 7916:   val=    Must be a column whose cells contain numbers (after stripping
 7917:           currency symbols, commas, and handling accounting-notation negatives).
 7918:           Rows where the value is still non-numeric after stripping are skipped.
 7919:           Negative totals are included in the chart (slice values may be negative
 7920:           if the source data contains credits or reversals).
 7921: 
 7922:   donut=  Any truthy value (e.g. "1") enables the donut hole.  Absent or
 7923:           "0" renders a solid pie.
 7924: 
 7925: =head3 MESSAGES
 7926: 
 7927:   Missing cat or val column parameter    C<cat=> or C<val=> query param absent.
 7928:   Column not found: <name>               Named column absent from the result set.
 7929:   Could not open data source             Left-table spec unresolvable or 404.
 7930:   No plottable data: ...                 All rows lack a valid numeric value.
 7931: 
 7932: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7933: 
 7934:   GET /pie?l=table:sales&cat=region&val=amount&back=/view/sales
 7935: 
 7936: Renders a pie chart of total C<amount> per C<region>.  Clicking the
 7937: "North" slice navigates to C</view/sales?f=region:eq:North>.
 7938: 
 7939: With a donut hole:
 7940: 
 7941:   GET /pie?l=table:sales&cat=region&val=amount&donut=1
 7942: 
 7943: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 7944: 
 7945:   pie_view : Controller x Params -> HTML | Error
 7946: 
 7947:   let pipeline = run_export_pipeline(l, j, f, d)
 7948:   let totals   = { cat => sum { val(row) | row in pipeline.records,
 7949:                                            cat(row) != '', num(val(row)) != bot }
 7950:                  | cat in range(cat_col) }
 7951: 
 7952:   pre  cat in params /\ val in params     else 400
 7953:   pre  cat in pipeline.cols               else 400
 7954:   pre  val in pipeline.cols               else 400
 7955:   pre  totals != {}                        else 200 "No plottable data"
 7956:   post HTML::D3->render_pie_chart_snippet(slices) embedded in TT layout
 7957:        where slices = sort_desc { |s.value| | s in totals }
 7958: 
 7959: =cut
 7960: 
 7961: =head2 clear_uploads
 7962: 
 7963: C<POST /uploads/clear> -- Delete every file from the C<.uploads/> staging
 7964: directory and return the total disk space recovered.
 7965: 
 7966: The C<.uploads/> directory accumulates files from drag-and-drop uploads.
 7967: It grows indefinitely; this action lets users reclaim that space without
 7968: needing shell access.
 7969: 
 7970: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7971: 
 7972: =head4 INPUT
 7973: 
 7974: No parameters.  The C<.uploads/> path is always the one under C<app->home>.
 7975: 
 7976: =head4 OUTPUT
 7977: 
 7978:   200 application/json   { "freed": <bytes>, "count": <n> }
 7979: 
 7980: C<freed> is the total number of bytes removed.  C<count> is the number of
 7981: files deleted.  Both are 0 when the directory is absent or already empty.
 7982: 
 7983: =cut
 7984: 
 7985: =head2 scatter_view
 7986: 
 7987: C<GET /scatter> -- Render a D3.js scatter plot or bubble chart.
 7988: 
 7989: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 7990: 
 7991:   x=<col>        X-axis column (required; numeric)
 7992:   y=<col>        Y-axis column (required; numeric)
 7993:   series=<col>   Colour-by category column (optional)
 7994:   size=<col>     Bubble-radius numeric column (optional)
 7995:   scheme=<name>  D3 colour scheme (default tableau10; only used with series=)
 7996:   min_r=N        Minimum bubble radius in px (default 3; range 1-40)
 7997:   max_r=N        Maximum bubble radius in px (default 15; range 1-40)
 7998:   back=<url>     URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 7999: 
 8000: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 8001: 
 8002: =head4 INPUT
 8003: 
 8004:   l        spec    Required.  Left table spec (table:name or path:/abs/path).
 8005:   x        string  Required.  Numeric X-axis column name.
 8006:   y        string  Required.  Numeric Y-axis column name.
 8007:   series   string  Optional.  Categorical colour-dimension column.
 8008:   size     string  Optional.  Numeric bubble-radius column.
 8009:   scheme   string  Optional.  One of: tableau10 category10 set2 set3 paired.
 8010:   min_r    int     Optional.  Minimum radius 1-40 (default 3).
 8011:   max_r    int     Optional.  Maximum radius 1-40 (default 15).
 8012:   back     url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL (sanitised by _safe_back_url).
 8013: 
 8014: =head4 OUTPUT
 8015: 
 8016:   200 text/html    Rendered scatter chart page.
 8017:   400 text/plain   Missing x= or y= parameter.
 8018:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 8019:   400 text/plain   Unknown colour scheme: <scheme>  (only when series= is set).
 8020:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source" when l= is absent/invalid.
 8021: 
 8022: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 8023: 
 8024:   scatter_view == lambda self .
 8025:     pre  length(x_col) > 0 /\ length(y_col) > 0
 8026:          /\ x_col in columns /\ y_col in columns
 8027:          /\ (series_col != '' => series_col in columns)
 8028:          /\ (size_col   != '' => size_col   in columns)
 8029:          /\ (series_col != '' => scheme in SCATTER_SCHEMES)
 8030:          /\ 1 <= min_r <= 40 /\ 1 <= max_r <= 40
 8031:     post render(scatter, pairs)
 8032: 
 8033: =head3 EXAMPLE
 8034: 
 8035:   GET /scatter?l=table:sales&x=quantity&y=amount&series=region
 8036:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 scatter chart SVG
 8037: 
 8038: =cut
 8039: 
 8040: =head2 stacked_view
 8041: 
 8042: C<GET /stacked> -- Render a D3.js stacked bar chart.
 8043: 
 8044: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 8045: 
 8046:   cat=<col>     X-axis category column (required)
 8047:   series=<col>  Stack-segment column (required; seg= is a backwards-compat alias)
 8048:   val=<col>     Numeric measure column (optional; count rows when absent)
 8049:   scheme=<name> D3 colour scheme (default tableau10)
 8050:   back=<url>    URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 8051: 
 8052: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 8053: 
 8054: =head4 INPUT
 8055: 
 8056:   l       spec    Required.  Left table spec.
 8057:   cat     string  Required.  X-axis categorical column name.
 8058:   series  string  Required.  Stack-segment categorical column name.
 8059:   val     string  Optional.  Numeric measure column; omit for count mode.
 8060:   scheme  string  Optional.  D3 colour scheme name.
 8061:   back    url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL.
 8062: 
 8063: =head4 OUTPUT
 8064: 
 8065:   200 text/html    Rendered stacked bar chart page.
 8066:   400 text/plain   Missing cat= or series= parameter.
 8067:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 8068:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source".
 8069: 
 8070: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 8071: 
 8072:   stacked_view == lambda self .
 8073:     pre  length(cat_col) > 0 /\ length(series_col) > 0
 8074:          /\ cat_col in columns /\ series_col in columns
 8075:     let rows = _grouped_totals(records, cat_col, series_col, val_col) in
 8076:     post render(stacked, rows)
 8077: 
 8078: =head3 EXAMPLE
 8079: 
 8080:   GET /stacked?l=table:sales&cat=region&series=product&val=amount
 8081:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 stacked bar chart SVG
 8082: 
 8083: =cut

 8085: =head2 combo_view
 8086: 
 8087: C<GET /combo> -- Render a D3.js combo bar+line chart.
 8088: 
 8089: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 8090: 
 8091:   cat=<col>       X-axis category column (required; x= is a backwards-compat alias)
 8092:   bar_val=<col>   Numeric column plotted as bars (required; bar= is a backwards-compat alias)
 8093:   line_val=<col>  Numeric column plotted as a line (required; line= is a backwards-compat alias)
 8094:   back=<url>      URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 8095: 
 8096: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 8097: 
 8098: =head4 INPUT
 8099: 
 8100:   l         spec    Required.  Left table spec.
 8101:   cat       string  Required.  X-axis categorical column name (x= also accepted).
 8102:   bar_val   string  Required.  Numeric bar-measure column (bar= also accepted).
 8103:   line_val  string  Required.  Numeric line-measure column (line= also accepted).
 8104:   back      url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL.
 8105: 
 8106: =head4 OUTPUT
 8107: 
 8108:   200 text/html    Rendered combo chart page.
 8109:   400 text/plain   Missing cat=, bar_val=, or line_val= parameter.
 8110:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 8111:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source".
 8112: 
 8113: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 8114: 
 8115:   combo_view == lambda self .
 8116:     pre  length(cat_col) > 0 /\ length(bar_col) > 0 /\ length(line_col) > 0
 8117:          /\ cat_col in columns /\ bar_col in columns /\ line_col in columns
 8118:     post render(combo, pairs)
 8119: 
 8120: =head3 EXAMPLE
 8121: 
 8122:   GET /combo?l=table:sales&cat=month&bar_val=revenue&line_val=units
 8123:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 combo bar+line chart SVG

 8126: 
 8127: =head2 treemap_view
 8128: 
 8129: C<GET /treemap> -- Render a D3.js treemap where tile area encodes a numeric column.
 8130: 
 8131: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 8132: 
 8133:   group=<col>  Tile-label categorical column (required; cat= is a backwards-compat alias)
 8134:   size=<col>   Tile-area numeric column (required; val= is a backwards-compat alias)
 8135:   topn=N       Keep only the top-N tiles by size; remainder collapsed into "Other"
 8136:   scheme=<name> D3 colour scheme (default tableau10)
 8137:   back=<url>   URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 8138: 
 8139: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 8140: 
 8141: =head4 INPUT
 8142: 
 8143:   l       spec    Required.  Left table spec.
 8144:   group   string  Required.  Tile-label categorical column (cat= also accepted).
 8145:   size    string  Required.  Tile-area numeric column (val= also accepted).
 8146:   topn    int     Optional.  Keep top-N tiles; 0 or absent means no limit.
 8147:   scheme  string  Optional.  D3 colour scheme name.
 8148:   back    url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL.
 8149: 
 8150: =head4 OUTPUT
 8151: 
 8152:   200 text/html    Rendered treemap page.
 8153:   400 text/plain   Missing group= or size= parameter.
 8154:   400 text/plain   Unrecognised scheme= parameter (?color= returns 400).
 8155:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 8156:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source".
 8157: 
 8158: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 8159: 
 8160:   treemap_view == lambda self .
 8161:     pre  length(group_col) > 0 /\ length(size_col) > 0
 8162:          /\ group_col in columns /\ size_col in columns
 8163:          /\ color param absent (scheme= is the correct param name)
 8164:     let tiles = _grouped_totals(records, group_col, [], size_sum) in
 8165:     let top   = topn > 0 ? top_n(tiles, topn) : tiles in
 8166:     post render(treemap, top)
 8167: 
 8168: =head3 EXAMPLE
 8169: 
 8170:   GET /treemap?l=table:sales&group=product&size=amount&topn=10
 8171:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 treemap SVG
 8172: 
 8173: =cut
 8174: 
 8175: =head2 histogram_view
 8176: 
 8177: C<GET /histogram> -- Render a D3.js histogram with server-side binning.
 8178: 
 8179: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 8180: 
 8181:   col=<col>   Numeric column to bin (required)
 8182:   bins=N      Number of bins (default 10; max 200)
 8183:   back=<url>  URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 8184: 
 8185: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 8186: 
 8187: =head4 INPUT
 8188: 
 8189:   l     spec    Required.  Left table spec.
 8190:   col   string  Required.  Numeric column to histogram.
 8191:   bins  int     Optional.  Bin count 1-200 (default 10).
 8192:   back  url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL.
 8193: 
 8194: =head4 OUTPUT
 8195: 
 8196:   200 text/html    Rendered histogram page.
 8197:   400 text/plain   Missing col= parameter.
 8198:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 8199:   200 text/html    Empty histogram page when no numeric values exist.
 8200:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source".
 8201: 
 8202: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 8203: 
 8204:   histogram_view == lambda self .
 8205:     pre  length(col) > 0 /\ col in columns
 8206:          /\ 1 <= bins <= 200
 8207:     let vals  = { row[col] | row in records, looks_like_number(row[col]) } in
 8208:     let width = (max(vals) - min(vals)) / bins in
 8209:     let bdata = bin(vals, width) in
 8210:     post render(histogram, bdata, bins => |bdata|)
 8211: 
 8212: =head3 EXAMPLE
 8213: 
 8214:   GET /histogram?l=table:sales&col=amount&bins=20
 8215:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 histogram SVG
 8216: 
 8217: =cut
 8218: 
 8219: =head2 map_view
 8220: 
 8221: C<GET /map> -- Render an OpenStreetMap map of the view.
 8222: 
 8223: A row gets a position one of two ways.  With C<loc=E<lt>columnE<gt>> the column
 8224: holds places, and each distinct value is geocoded once by
 8225: L<Database::BI::Model::Geocode> (one lookup per value however often the column
 8226: repeats it); the answers are remembered in the model store and shared by every
 8227: dataset, so a second visit - or another file naming the same place - asks
 8228: nothing.  With C<lat=> and C<lon=> the columns already hold
 8229: coordinates.  Rows sharing a position become one marker whose popup says how
 8230: many rows it stands for.  Markers are clustered with Leaflet.markercluster, so
 8231: a dense map draws a count at low zoom and splits it into individual pins as it
 8232: is zoomed in; a sparse map looks the same, because markers far enough apart are
 8233: left on their own.
 8234: 
 8235: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<c=> and C<f=> pipeline params as every other
 8236: visual, so the map shows the filtered, calculated view.
 8237: 
 8238:   loc=<col>      Location column to geocode (required unless lat= and lon= are given)
 8239:   lat=<col>      Latitude column (numeric, -90..90)
 8240:   lon=<col>      Longitude column (numeric, -180..180)
 8241:   popup=<col>    Column shown in the marker popup (repeatable; default: none)
 8242:   max=N          Markers to draw (default: map_max_markers, 2000)
 8243:   back=<url>     URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 8244: 
 8245: Config values: C<map_geocode_max> (new lookups per request, default 500),
 8246: C<map_max_markers> (markers per request, default 2000), C<map_region> (a
 8247: two-letter country hint for ambiguous place names) and C<geocoder> (an object to
 8248: use instead of loading L<Geo::Coder::Free>; the seam the tests use).
 8249: 
 8250: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 8251: 
 8252: =head4 INPUT
 8253: 
 8254:   l        spec    Required.  Left table spec (table:name or path:/abs/path).
 8255:   loc      string  Optional.  Column of places to geocode.
 8256:   lat      string  Optional.  Numeric latitude column.
 8257:   lon      string  Optional.  Numeric longitude column.
 8258:   popup    string  Optional, repeatable.  Columns shown in the popup.
 8259:   max      int     Optional.  Positive marker cap for this request.
 8260:   back     url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL (sanitised by _safe_back_url).
 8261: 
 8262: =head4 OUTPUT
 8263: 
 8264:   200 text/html    Rendered map page.
 8265:   400 text/plain   No loc=, or lat= without lon=.
 8266:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>  (or the geocode message naming it).
 8267:   400 text/plain   Coordinate column wholly out of range, naming it.
 8268:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source" when l= is absent/invalid.
 8269: 
 8270: =head3 EXAMPLE
 8271: 
 8272:   GET /map?l=table:sales&loc=place
 8273:     -> 200 HTML page containing Leaflet markers for the places that resolved
 8274: 
 8275: =cut
 8276: 
 8277: =head2 calc_list / calc_save / calc_delete
 8278: 
 8279: C<GET /calc/list>, C<POST /calc/save>, C<POST /calc/delete> -- Manage stored
 8280: calculated fields for a table spec.
 8281: 
 8282: Calculated fields are named expression strings evaluated per-row by
 8283: C<Database::BI::Calc> and appended as virtual columns before any aggregation.
 8284: 
 8285: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 8286: 
 8287: =head4 calc_list (GET /calc/list)
 8288: 
 8289:   INPUT:  l=<spec>   (required)
 8290:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "fields": { "<name>": "<expr>", ... } }
 8291:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 8292: 
 8293: =head4 calc_save (POST /calc/save)
 8294: 
 8295:   INPUT:  l=<spec>   (required)
 8296:           name=<id>  (required; must match /\A[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*\z/)
 8297:           expr=<expression>  (required; max 512 chars; hostile patterns rejected)
 8298:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "saved": 1 }
 8299:           400 application/json  { "error": "..." }  (compile error or hostile input)
 8300: 
 8301: =head4 calc_delete (POST /calc/delete)
 8302: 
 8303:   INPUT:  l=<spec>   (required)
 8304:           name=<id>  (required)
 8305:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "deleted": 1 }
 8306:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 8307: 
 8308: =head3 EXAMPLE
 8309: 
 8310:   POST /calc/save  l=path:/data/sales.csv&name=margin&expr=amount-cost
 8311:     -> 200 { "saved": 1 }
 8312:   GET /calc/list?l=path:/data/sales.csv
 8313:     -> 200 { "fields": { "margin": "amount-cost" } }
 8314: 
 8315: =cut
 8316: 
 8317: =head2 groups_list / groups_save / groups_delete
 8318: 
 8319: C<GET /groups/list>, C<POST /groups/save>, C<POST /groups/delete> -- Manage
 8320: named value groups that produce virtual C<${col}_group> columns.
 8321: 
 8322: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 8323: 
 8324: =head4 groups_list (GET /groups/list)
 8325: 
 8326:   INPUT:  l=<spec>  (required), col=<col>  (required)
 8327:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "groups": { "<label>": ["<val>", ...], ... } }
 8328:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" } or "col= required"
 8329: 
 8330: =head4 groups_save (POST /groups/save)
 8331: 
 8332:   INPUT:  l=<spec>, col=<col>, group=<label>, values=<newline-separated values>
 8333:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "saved": 1 }
 8334:           400 application/json  { "error": "..." }
 8335: 
 8336: =head4 groups_delete (POST /groups/delete)
 8337: 
 8338:   INPUT:  l=<spec>, col=<col>, group=<label>  (omit group= to delete all groups for col)
 8339:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "deleted": 1 }
 8340:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 8341: 
 8342: =head3 EXAMPLE
 8343: 
 8344:   POST /groups/save  l=path:/data/sales.csv&col=region&group=North&values=MN%0AWI%0AIA
 8345:     -> 200 { "saved": 1 }
 8346:   GET /groups/list?l=path:/data/sales.csv&col=region
 8347:     -> 200 { "groups": { "North": ["MN","WI","IA"] } }
 8348: 
 8349: =cut
 8350: 
 8351: =head2 dates_list / dates_save / dates_delete
 8352: 
 8353: C<GET /dates/list>, C<POST /dates/save>, C<POST /dates/delete> -- Manage stored
 8354: date-normalisation patterns that convert non-ISO date columns to ISO-8601
 8355: (C<YYYY-MM-DD>) in-place before aggregation.
 8356: 
 8357: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 8358: 
 8359: =head4 dates_list (GET /dates/list)
 8360: 
 8361:   INPUT:  l=<spec>  (required)
 8362:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "patterns": { "<col>": "<pattern>", ... } }
 8363:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 8364: 
 8365: =head4 dates_save (POST /dates/save)
 8366: 
 8367:   INPUT:  l=<spec>, col=<col>, pattern=<fmt>
 8368:           Supported patterns: %m/%d/%Y  %d/%m/%Y  %d-%b-%Y
 8369:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "ok": 1 }
 8370:           400 application/json  { "error": "Unsupported date format pattern ..." }
 8371: 
 8372: =head4 dates_delete (POST /dates/delete)
 8373: 
 8374:   INPUT:  l=<spec>, col=<col>  (omit col= to delete all patterns for the spec)
 8375:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "deleted": 1 }
 8376:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 8377: 
 8378: =head3 EXAMPLE
 8379: 
 8380:   POST /dates/save  l=path:/data/sales.csv&col=sale_date&pattern=%m/%d/%Y
 8381:     -> 200 { "ok": 1 }
 8382:   GET /dates/list?l=path:/data/sales.csv
 8383:     -> 200 { "patterns": { "sale_date": "%m/%d/%Y" } }
 8384: 
 8385: =cut
 8386: 
 8387: =head1 DESCRIPTION
 8388: 
 8389: All user-facing routes in C<Database::BI> are handled by this controller.
 8390: See the individual action POD above for per-endpoint documentation.
 8391: 
 8392: =head2 Filter operators and the quick-filter bar
 8393: 
 8394: Filters are expressed as C<f=col:op:val> query parameters and are applied
 8395: server-side after all joins.  Multiple C<f=> params are applied in order
 8396: (AND semantics).
 8397: 
 8398: The colon separator is split with a limit of 3, so values may themselves
 8399: contain colons (e.g. C<f=sale_date:eq:2025-01-15>, or a time value like
 8400: C<f=start_time:eq:14:30:00>).
 8401: 
 8402: B<Supported operators:>
 8403: 
 8404:   eq        case-insensitive string equality
 8405:   ne        case-insensitive string inequality
 8406:   contains  case-insensitive substring match
 8407:   starts    case-insensitive prefix match
 8408:   lt        numeric less-than
 8409:   le        numeric less-than-or-equal
 8410:   gt        numeric greater-than
 8411:   ge        numeric greater-than-or-equal
 8412:   empty     cell is undef or empty string (val ignored)
 8413:   notempty  cell is defined and non-empty (val ignored)
 8414: 
 8415: B<Quick-filter bar (table view):>
 8416: 
 8417: A filter row below the toolbar lets the user pick a column, operator, and
 8418: value and press "Apply filter" (or Enter) to add a condition without
 8419: opening any panel.  The current URL is updated with the new C<f=> param
 8420: and the page reloads; all other URL parameters (joins, dedup, etc.) are
 8421: preserved automatically via the browser C<URL> API.
 8422: 
 8423: Active filter chips appear in the toolbar.  Each chip has an individual
 8424: x-button to remove just that condition.  The "x Clear filters" link
 8425: removes all C<f=> params at once.
 8426: 
 8427: B<Drill-down from pie chart:>
 8428: 
 8429: Clicking a pie slice or legend entry on C</pie> navigates to the table
 8430: view with C<f=cat_col:eq:label> appended, so the user can inspect the
 8431: rows behind any slice without manually typing a filter.
 8432: 
 8433: =head1 COMMON PITFALLS
 8434: 
 8435: These are the most common mistakes when working with this controller.
 8436: 
 8437: =over 4
 8438: 
 8439: =item B<SQLite files must use the .sql extension, not .sqlite>
 8440: 
 8441: C<Database::Abstraction> (the data-reading library) probes for a file called
 8442: C<tablename.sql> when it wants to open a SQLite database.  It does B<not>
 8443: look for C<.sqlite> or C<.db3>.  If your file is called C<inventory.sqlite>,
 8444: rename it to C<inventory.sql> or it will not appear in the file browser and
 8445: will return 404 when opened.
 8446: 
 8447: =item B<Filter values that contain a colon still work>
 8448: 
 8449: A date like C<2025-01-15> contains hyphens, not colons, so it is fine.  But
 8450: if your value itself contains a colon (for example, a time like C<14:30:00>),
 8451: the filter still works because the C<col:op:val> spec is split on the B<first
 8452: two> colons only -- the rest of the string becomes the value.
 8453: 
 8454:   # This correctly matches "14:30:00" in the start_time column:
 8455:   ?f=start_time:eq:14:30:00
 8456: 
 8457: =item B<Left join keeps only the FIRST matching right-table row>
 8458: 
 8459: When the right table has two rows with the same join key, only the first one
 8460: (in file order) is used.  The second is silently ignored.  If you need all
 8461: matches, consider pre-processing your data so join keys are unique.
 8462: 
 8463: =item B<Export format comes from the filename extension, not a Content-Type header>
 8464: 
 8465: When using C<POST /export> to save a file to disk, the format (CSV or SQLite)
 8466: is determined by the extension of the C<filename> parameter.  C<.csv> produces
 8467: a CSV file; C<.sql> produces a SQLite database.  Any other extension returns
 8468: HTTP 415 (Unsupported Media Type).  The C<Content-Type> request header is
 8469: ignored entirely.
 8470: 
 8471: =item B<Template Toolkit variables starting with underscore are silently dropped>
 8472: 
 8473: If you add a stash variable with a name starting with C<_> (for example,
 8474: C<_tmp> or C<_result>), Template Toolkit will silently produce an empty string
 8475: when the template tries to read it.  This is a TT quirk when C<TRIM =E<gt> 1>
 8476: is active.  Always use names that start with a letter.
 8477: 
 8478: =item B<_apply_filter_spec always passes all records through for unknown operators>
 8479: 
 8480: If you pass an operator that is not in the supported list (for example C<regex>
 8481: or C<like>), the filter is treated as a no-op and B<all rows are returned>.  No
 8482: error is produced.  This is intentional so that future operators can be added
 8483: without breaking existing clients that read a wider response.
 8484: 
 8485: =item B<Uploaded files are evicted automatically, but not immediately>
 8486: 
 8487: Files uploaded via C<POST /upload> are stored in C<.uploads/> under the
 8488: application home directory.  C<Database::BI> evicts upload subdirectories
 8489: whose modification time is older than 24 hours B<on every server startup>.
 8490: Between restarts, files accumulate.  For an immediate full purge (regardless
 8491: of age), post to C<POST /uploads/clear> or click the "Clear upload cache"
 8492: button in the UI.
 8493: 
 8494: =item B<Open C<data/> tables by name; open other files by absolute path>
 8495: 
 8496: The C<view> action (C<GET /view/:table>) only looks inside C<data_dir>.
 8497: To open a file from anywhere else on the filesystem, use C<open_file>
 8498: (C<GET /open?path=/abs/path>).  The two routes use different URL schemes and
 8499: are not interchangeable.
 8500: 
 8501: =back
 8502: 
 8503: =head1 LIMITATIONS
 8504: 
 8505: =over 4
 8506: 
 8507: =item *
 8508: 
 8509: Multi-table joins are delegated to C<Database::Join> with C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'>.
 8510: For datasets up to C<join_max_rows> combined rows (default 10,000; configurable
 8511: in C<database_bi.conf>) the join runs in Perl memory; larger datasets spill to
 8512: a temporary SQLite database so peak RAM is bounded by the result set rather than
 8513: the sum of all source tables.
 8514: 
 8515: =item *
 8516: 
 8517: C<_resolve_language> extracts only the primary language subtag from the first
 8518: C<Accept-Language> tag (e.g. C<de> from C<de-DE,de;q=0.9,en;q=0.8>).
 8519: Quality weights and multiple alternatives are not ranked.
 8520: 
 8521: =item *
 8522: 
 8523: No GeoIP-based language resolution is implemented.  The language is resolved
 8524: from the HTTP C<Accept-Language> header only.
 8525: 
 8526: =item *
 8527: 
 8528: C<Sub::Protected> enforcement requires the CHECK compilation phase; when modules
 8529: are loaded dynamically at test time the "Too late to run CHECK block" warning
 8530: is emitted and the restrilanguage> extracts only the primary language subtag from the first
 8531: C<Accept-Language> tag (e.g. C<de> from C<de-DE,de;q=0.9,en;q=0.8>).
 8532: Quality weights and multiple alternatives are not ranked.
 8533: 
 8534: =item *
 8535: 
 8536: No GeoIP-based language resolution is implemented.  The language is resolved
 8537: from the HTTP C<Accept-Language> header only.
 8538: 
 8539: =item *
 8540: 
 8541: C<Sub::Protected> enforcement requires the CHECK compilation phase; when modules
 8542: are loaded dynamically at test time the "Too late to run CHECK block" warning
 8543: is emitted and the restriction is not enforced in that context.
 8544: 
 8545: =back
